Remarks.&mdash;During October the highest reading of the barometer was 30.330, on the 21st; and the lowest 29.576, on the 5th. The highest temperature was 89&deg;, on the 4th and 5th; the lowest 63&deg;, on the 30th ; and the mean during month was 750.7. N.E. winds prevailed, and the strongest was recorded on the 5th, averaging 26.3 miles an hour during 24 hours. Rain fell on 2 days, measuring 0.40 inch. During November the highest reading of the barometer was 30.422, on the 23rd; and the lowest 30.000, on the 20th. The highest temperature was 82&deg;, on the 28th, 29th, and 30th; the lowest 56&deg;, 011 the 13th and 14th; and the mean during month was 7o&deg;.7. N.E. winds prevailed, and the strongest was recorded 011 the 22nd, averaging 14 miles an hour during 24 hours. Rain fell on 2 days, measuring 0.03 inch. During December the highest reading of the barometer was 30.578, 011 the 18th; and the lowest 30.092, on the 6th. The highest temperature was 770, on the 2nd, 3rd, and 25th; the lowest 440, on the 18th; and the mean during month was 66&deg;.7. N.E. winds prevailed, and the strongest was recorded on the 16th, averaging 14.6 miles an hour during 24 hours. Rain fell on 5 days, measuring 0.67 inch. IX. 194 CUSTOMS GAZETTE. [OCT.-DEC., (Received iSth January 1895J CUSTOM HOUSE, KOWLOON, 1 ith January 1895. SIR, I.&mdash;a. In conformity with the instructions contained in your Circular No. 381, Second Series, I now transmit to the Statistical Department at Shanghai, for publication, the statistics of the trade at Kowloon and the neighbouring Stations in Native vessels for the fourth quarter of the year 1894, October-December. b. The Custom House a 1 id was opened on the 2nd April 1887, and the collection of Provincial Likin and Ching-fei Tax on General Cargo, at the rates fixed by the Provincial Tariff received from the Viceroy of the Two Kwang, began on that day. The collection of Tariff Import Duty and Convention Likin on Opium (Hk.Th 30 and Hk.Th 80 per picul) commenced on the 14th April 1887, and the collection of Native Duties on General Cargo, at the rates fixed by the Native Custom House Tariff received from the Hoppo, on the ist July 1887. The stations at present controlled by the Kowloon Customs are known as the Capsuimoon &amp;gt;$C P^)&amp;gt; Changchow (J| $[&#39;|), Fotochow &bull;(Mm and Kowloon &sect;|) stations. c. The word &quot;Inwards&quot; in these statistics means to China, and the word &quot; Outwards,&quot; from China. II. 1894.] LAPPA. 205 (Received 18th January 1895.) ■&raquo; CUSTOM HOUSE, &bull; LAPPA, 9TH January 1895. SIR, I.&mdash;a. In conformity with the instructions contained in your Circular No. 381, Second Series, I now transmit to the Statistical Department at Shanghai, for publication, the statistics of the trade of this port for the fourth quarter of the year 1894, October-December. b. The Custom House Jjfc HD) was opened on the 2nd April 1887. The collection of Tariff Import Duty and Convention Likin 011 Opium (111:.&#39;TU. 30 and HI:.lis. 80 per picul) and the collection of Provincial Likin and Ching-fei Tax on General Cargo, at the rates fixed by the Provincial Tariff received from the Viceroy of the Two Kwang, com&not; menced on that day. The collection of Native Duties on General Cargo, at the rates fixed by the Native Custom House Tariff received from the Hoppo, commenced on the ist July 1887. The stations at present controlled by the Lappa Customs are known as the Malowchow (M fSi #H) and Chienshan (}jij III) stations, with three sub-stations, known as the Shekkok Ivuttai (&quot;jpf ft), and Kwanchiap (&sect;| ftp]) sub-stations. c. The word &quot; Inwards &quot; in these statistics means to China, and the word &quot; Outwards,&quot; from China. II.
